# Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile

- **Aliases:** Meetra, Surik, Jedi Exile, the Exile
- **Era:** KOTOR II
- **Continuity:** default Legends; entries present for: Legends
- **Research tier:** DEEP
- **Entries:** 1161 (817 with quotations; 238 marked as fragments; recounted after the public-release cleanup). Rebuilt 2026-11-17 from 1452 old entries; old file: `characters/_bak/meetra-surik.md.bak-20261117`.
- **How to read:** each entry is the quotation in full (block quote), then its source and point of view. "Note" lines are summaries from our notes, not quotations. `[fragment — full passage not on file]` means the source text is not on the box, so only the words in our notes are shown. Debate and forum material is in the last section and is unverified.

Note (our notes, around the quote): The Exile's trial. Kavar: «it was as if the Force had died within you, leaving you hollow» [K2/Royal_Palace]. Vash, in the Korriban vision: «The dark side is not what I sensed in the exile.» [K2/Lost_Jedi]. Zez-Kai Ell left the Order the day of the exile [K2/Goto's_Yacht]. > The pain, however, was unexpected
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: K2/Hangar_Bay — Star Wars Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords (KOTOR 2): power-scaling notes (Oct 19, 2026). POV: speaker not named in our notes.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Peragus. The Exile wakes in the medbay. The miners were killed by their own droids, steered by HK-50, the "maintenance officer". Atton is found locked up in the security block. In the dormitories the Exile hears Kreia's voice through the bond. The asteroid explodes after the escape on the Ebon Hawk. Earlier, aboard the Harbinger, Kreia confronts Sion, loses a hand, and the Exile feels her pain through the bond: «The pain, however, was unexpected» [K2/Hangar_Bay].
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/kotor-2-notes.md:212`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Telos Citadel Station. The Exchange (Luxa, Loppak Slusk) and Czerka's Jana Lorso set up a side choice between the Ithorians (Chodo Habat) and Czerka over the restoration contract. TSF Lt. Grenn and Admiral Onasi appear. The Exile is held by the TSF; the party's gear is recovered from "TSF detainee lockers" (walkthrough narration), and the Ebon Hawk is later reclaimed. Bao-Dur repairs the shuttle. On the Polar Plateau the party fights HK-50s. At the Secret Academy the Exile meets Atris and her Handmaidens. Atris turns down the Exile's offer of help and sends them to find the remaining Masters. Depending on the Exile's gender, the Handmaiden (male Exile) or the Disciple (female Exile) joins the crew.
  — Source: Star Wars Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords (KOTOR 2): power-scaling notes (Oct 19, 2026). POV: speaker not named in our notes.
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/kotor-2-notes.md:213`

Zez-Kai Ell turns out to have been in hiding as a refugee; Mira says he «hired me to watch out for you» [K2/Goto's_Yacht]. > he drops to his knees *(narration)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Star Wars Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords (KOTOR 2): power-scaling notes (Oct 19, 2026). POV: speaker not named in our notes.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Malachor V. The Ebon Hawk crashes. The party splits up: Mira vs Hanharr in the Depths, Bao-Dur's remote vs G0-T0, HK-47 vs HK-50s. In the Trayus Academy Sion is beaten four times. After the fourth defeat "he drops to his knees" (narration); the persuasion lines break his will. In the Trayus Core the Exile faces Kreia, who gives prophecies for each companion, then fights her with three floating lightsabers and kills her. The remote fires the mass shadow generator. The ending branches on alignment (the walkthrough uses an Alignment-40 threshold). Exact ending-text wording was not verified line by line.
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/kotor-2-notes.md:221`

Note (our notes, around the quote): KEY FEAT AND LIMITATION (narration, Ravager bridge): "Nihilus raises his hand to drain you, but suddenly reels back, collapsing on the ground". Visas can disrupt the link so that "Nihilus is incapacitated for one combat round". His drain backfires on the Exile.

> As the ship drew closer to the dingy brown world she felt a growing sense of unease and discomfort. In some ways it reminded her of Malachor V—the massive and instantaneous loss of life on that doomed world had created a wound in the Force. The activation of the mass-shadow generator had obliterated two armies, shredding apart the bonds of the Force that linked all living things.
— Source: Star Wars Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords (KOTOR 2): power-scaling notes (Oct 19, 2026). POV: speaker not named in our notes.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Revan (Drew Karpyshyn), Chapter Eighteen (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Old_Republic_Series_Star_Wars_Old_Republic_03_Revan_Drew_Karpyshyn_epub`).)
(Fragment completed 2026-11-17: full sentence/passage matched word for word in the source text on file: `MEGA_Legend_The_Old_Republic_Series_Star_Wars_Old_Republic_03_Revan_Drew_Karpyshyn_epub`.)
Note (our notes, around the quote): The Exile (Meetra Surik). Feats: in-game, kills Nihilus with the party, beats Sion repeatedly and then breaks his will, defeats Atris, defeats Traya, and (on the dark path) kills Kavar, Vrook and Zez. Statements: see D3. Arc: from a hollow outcast to someone reconnected to the Force; "a wound in the Force" that feeds through bonds. Verdict implications: the in-game record is the strongest of any K2 character, but it comes with party and bond assistance. Medium confidence for a neutral-ground ranking.
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/kotor-2-notes.md:269`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Darth Nihilus. Feats: destroyed Katarr; the Ravager and its fleet. Weakness: Visas and Kreia indicate he can be undone by the Exile's bonds and "echo". His single on-screen fight is a loss. Verdict: hax/AoE power at the top, duel credentials unproven.
  — Source: Star Wars Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords (KOTOR 2): power-scaling notes (Oct 19, 2026).

(Fragment completed 2026-11-17: full sentence/passage matched word for word in the source text on file: `MEGA_Legend_The_Old_Republic_Series_Star_Wars_Old_Republic_03_Revan_Drew_Karpyshyn_epub`.)
Note text that was attached to the quote line: *(L6001)*
Note (our notes, around the quote): Feat vs. statement: Section 3 is what the narration *shows* (FEAT). Section 4 is what characters say or think (STATEMENT). Karpyshyn writes in rotating tight third person: Revan, Scourge, Meetra and Bastila each get scenes (the POV map is in §1b). So a lot of "narration" is that character's perception or estimate, and it is marked *(POV X)*. One distinction matters for scaling. POV *perception* (what someone senses) is fairly reliable. POV *self-assessment* or *prediction* («Alone, probably not», L5861; «the Emperor would have won regardless», L6129) is that character's opinion, not authorial fact. Karpyshyn does sometimes give an omniscient-style line inside a POV, e.g. «the Emperor was infinitely more powerful than Darth Nyriss had ever been» (L6001). I treat those as near-authorial because they explain an on-page result.
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/revan-novel-notes.md:10`

> In those days, he had believed this would protect him from the dark side, but he no longer had any such illusions. He was older and wiser. He understood that the two sides of the Force were more closely intertwined with each other than either the Jedi or the Sith would ever admit. He had learned to balance on the knife-edge between them, drawing on both the light and dark sides for strength.
— Source: L5397-5399 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Revan (Drew Karpyshyn), Chapter Twenty-Seven (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Old_Republic_Series_Star_Wars_Old_Republic_03_Revan_Drew_Karpyshyn_epub`).)
(Fragment completed 2026-11-17: full sentence/passage matched word for word in the source text on file: `MEGA_Legend_The_Old_Republic_Series_Star_Wars_Old_Republic_03_Revan_Drew_Karpyshyn_epub`.)
Note (our notes, around the quote): Headline: this is the Revan-meets-Vitiate book. It retcons the Mandalorian Wars and the Jedi Civil War into the Sith Emperor's plan: the Emperor manipulated Mandalore, then dominated Revan and Malak. The only Revan vs Emperor fight on page (Ch28) goes like this. Revan, with memories restored and «balance on the knife-edge», trades knockbacks with the Emperor and lands a redirected lightning bolt. Then he loses the lightning clash outright. T3 buys him time and dies. Meetra's Force-guided saber throw saves him and catches the Emperor «by surprise». Then Scourge stabs Meetra in the back after a Force vision, and the Emperor drops the distracted Revan with one bolt. Earlier, Revan Reborn redirects a dozen of Nyriss's bolts and burns her to ash in one exchange (Ch24). Nyriss had just had Scourge «barely» parrying and Meetra «clearly overmatched» (L5397-5399). The book's power ladder is clear: Emperor ≫ Revan Reborn > Nyriss ≥ Meetra ≈ Scourge > Xedrix (decrepit). The novel then turns Vitiate into a Revan-fed amp (Ch29), and gives Scourge his immortality.
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/revan-novel-notes.md:11`

Note (our notes, around the quote): R22 · Ch29 · L6173-6205 · [Stasis-Revan] ★ The mental war. He is physically paralyzed in stasis («even time itself could not touch him», L6173). He resists the Emperor's probes: «mentally he was strong enough to wage war against the Emperor, guarding and protecting his secrets for however long it might take» (L6195). He turns the link around: «The connection between them went both ways... he could subvert their relationship by planting seeds in the Emperor's thoughts» (L6197). He shapes the Emperor's policy: «Revan played on the Emperor's caution and patience... He augmented his irrational fear of death» (L6199). *Limits:* he is sustained by Meetra's spirit (L6179), and «it was inevitable that one day the Emperor would win their endless battle of wills» (L6205). *Shows:* top-tier mental resistance and influence against a planet-eater, and the source of the Emperor's amp.

> The affirmation of her own existence seemed to push the Void back, at least for the moment. But Meetra knew she couldn’t hold out against it much longer. >
> She could feel the Void pressing in on her from all sides. At the same time it was pulling on her, trying to rip away the very essence of her existence. Nature abhors a vacuum; the emptiness was trying to fill itself with her energy. For an instant she felt as if she were going to become undone, her physical body discorporating into trillions of subatomic particles that would scatter across the entire surface of Nathema.

> Alone, probably not. With the combined strength of Meetra, Scourge, and even T3, however, he believed they stood a real chance of victory.
>
> Betraying his allies had not altered the inevitable outcome; the Emperor would have won regardless. At least this way Scourge was still alive to carry on their cause.
>
> It was impossible to know what would have happened if Scourge had not betrayed them in the throne room. They might have lost anyway, but they also might have defeated the Emperor, forever freeing the galaxy from the threat of annihilation at the hands of a madman. There was no way to be sure, and no point in dwelling on the past.
>
> The Force washed over him in a wave, and a million possible futures flickered through his mind simultaneously. In some the Emperor was no more; in others he had transformed the entire galaxy into an empty wasteland. He saw both Revan’s triumph and defeat in the throne room; he saw variations of his own life and death played out over and over in every conceivable way, shape, and form.

Note (our notes, around the quote): (c) The 3-on-1 is never fought. Revan's POV beforehand: «With the combined strength of Meetra, Scourge, and even T3, however, he believed they stood a real chance of victory» (L5861). Afterward, Scourge thinks «the Emperor would have won regardless» (L6129), and Revan thinks «They might have lost anyway, but they also might have defeated the Emperor» (L6201). Scourge's Ch26 vision showed all three beaten (L5671-5687). That vision came before the betrayal decision, and it is ambiguous whether it was the future "with" the betrayal. The Ch28 million-futures vision shows Revan's «triumph and defeat in the throne room» both as possibilities (L6061). Net: the team-up was a live chance, the Emperor was favored, and the book deliberately leaves it open.
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/revan-novel-notes.md:391`

Note (our notes, around the quote): Note on MS9-MS10. Revan's «far greater than I could ever have imagined» measures her against *his expectations of her potential*, not against himself. «a path even I would not dare to tread» refers to her path (the Force-severing, Malachor, the Kreia lessons), not her rank. Neither line says "stronger than me". RS13 (Meetra's POV) explicitly ranks Revan above everyone she has met, and she has met Traya, Sion and Nihilus.
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/revan-novel-notes.md:479`

Note text that was attached to the quote line: *(L6037)*
Note (our notes, around the quote): Scaling adds: This book's Meetra is roughly Scourge-level and below Nyriss on page. That is a big gap from her KOTOR II ending, where she beat Sion, Nihilus (with help) and Traya. The novel never shows her signature Force-bond tricks (Force-breaking or draining). Her praise lines (MS9-MS10) are about growth and path, not rank. The book's most flattering *feat* for her is the Ch28 surprise disarm of the Emperor. Its most flattering *counterfactual* is Scourge's «she could have ended his life» (L6037). Both are qualified: a surprise move from outside the Emperor's focus, and an opinion.
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/revan-novel-notes.md:594`

What it shows (our notes): A sorceress, Dark Councilor for ~20 years, physically aged by choice (L457). Conspiratorial and cautious (she «refuses to take any real action», L3973). On page she 2v1s Scourge + Meetra with lightning, waves, barriers and fast saberwork. She is ashed by Revan in one exchange. She is the book's measuring stick: Revan Reborn ≫ Nyriss; the Emperor «infinitely» > Nyriss (L6001).
> “Nyriss still refuses to take any real action against the Emperor,” he grumbled.
>
> It felt good to say the words out loud. That was an unexpected benefit of being the only person who ever spoke to the prisoner. Anything he said in the cell would never leave these walls; here he could vent his frustrations aloud without fear of reprisal.
— Source: L457; L6001 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes.

She glanced over at T3 hovering anxiously near her in the cockpit, but the droid seemed unaffected. His lack of reaction merely reinforced the nature of her own suffering; as a droid, T3 could not sense the Force, and he wouldn’t notice if it was suddenly missing.

The rest of the Council is unnamed and appears only as an institution (retraining offer, hostility, the "new Jedi Council" in Part Two, L3635).

What it shows (our notes): L3511). Meetra (L3515). (L3615
> Led by a woman named Kreia, the rogue Jedi turned to the dark side teachings uncovered by Malak and Revan. Kreia took the name Darth Traya, and her followers called themselves the Sith after the long-lost species that had invaded the Republic a millennium before. They began a systemic purge of the galaxy, hunting down those who still held fast to the Jedi Code, killing them by the tens of thousands. Their relentless pursuit virtually wiped out the Order, and only those few who managed to flee or hide survived.
>
> Alone, she dared not challenge Darth Traya and her followers. Instead, she had focused on keeping her son alive. It had been the Exile—Meetra Surik—who had taken up the fight against the rogue Jedi. Three years after Revan’s unsuccessful attempt to locate her, she had emerged on her own to oppose and eventually defeat Darth Traya. Like Revan before her, she became the savior of the galaxy. And also as with Revan, there were many who felt her recent actions could not atone for the sins of her past.
>
> The Hawk limped back to Republic space; by the time it arrived, Traya and her followers had all but wiped out the Jedi. *(L3615)*
— Source: L3511; L3515; L3615 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes; What the book says.

> In those days, he had believed this would protect him from the dark side, but he no longer had any such illusions. He was older and wiser. He had learned to balance on the knife-edge between them, drawing on both the light and dark sides for strength.

> Revan was impressed by Scourge’s performance, not to mention relieved. On his last visit to Dromund Kaas, he and Malak had learned everything they could about the Imperial Guard. They were formidable opponents, even for a Jedi.

What it shows (our notes): Ch5 L1093 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Meetra, Mandalorian Wars general / post-Malachor (Force cut off), ~3,960 BBY [referenced]] «She felt the shock wave; it nearly killed her.»
> “Meetra was much closer to the mass-shadow generator than you were. She felt the shock wave; it nearly killed her. Left her vulnerable. At the same time, she felt the deaths of the Mandalorians and her fellow soldiers through the Force. It was all too much to bear in her weakened state. It would have killed her.” She paused for emphasis, before continuing. “Instinctively, she protected herself the only way she knew how. She cut herself off from the Force … permanently.”
— Source: The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes.

> She had visited Malachor V years after the cataclysm of the mass-shadow generator. Traversing its surface had been agony. Mentally, she had still sensed the anguish of all who had lost their lives there. Physically, the intense gravity of the world had held her in its crushing grip, leaving her gasping for breath. It had been the most awful and horrific experience of her life … until now.
— Source: The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes.

> Foolish as it was, Bastila couldn’t help but feel some hint of jealousy. Meetra had known Revan long before Bastila; she had answered his call to go to war against the Mandalorians, and in doing so she became one of his most trusted advisers and closest friends. Bastila knew they had shared a special bond not unlike that of Padawan and Master. Worst of all, Meetra was an integral part of Revan’s lost past—a past he had felt compelled to go in search of, even though it meant leaving his pregnant wife behind.
— Source: Ch17 · L3525 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Bastila), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.

> Through the cockpit window Meetra saw a path of destruction winding its way through the city below: the remnants of Revan’s crash landing. A massive chunk of permacrete had been smashed loose from a skyscraper passing by on the ship’s starboard side. The pavement of the street and sidewalk below had been torn up when the vessel had skipped and skidded down the thoroughfare. The mangled remnants of hovercars and speeders traced an irregular line down the street, the smaller vehicles crushed by the passage of the far more massive starship.
— Source: Ch18 · L3735 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Meetra), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.

What it shows (our notes): Ch18 · L3879 · POV Meetra · NARRATION · CAPTIVE Revan (drugged, amnesiac; ~3,951 BBY): «It was possible—and even likely—that the red-skinned being who had captured Revan had taken him to an entirely different planet, one she had never even heard of.»
> There were other possibilities to consider, as well. The Sith were an aggressive and war-like species; it was possible that Dromund Kaas was just one of many worlds in the Unknown Regions that had fallen under their control over the last thousand years. It was possible—and even likely—that the red-skinned being who had captured Revan had taken him to an entirely different planet, one she had never even heard of. But at least she had a lead. And no matter how slim the odds of finding her mentor, Meetra wasn’t about to give up. She trusted in the Force; eventually it would lead her to him.
— Source: Ch18 · L3879 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Meetra), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.

> Security guards, acolytes, and even civilian staff lay strewn about the halls and corridors; the Emperor's Guard had spared no one. Meetra understood there had been no other way to free Revan, but she still felt revulsion at the wholesale slaughter. When she noticed the body of a young Twi'lek slave lying on the floor with her throat cut, she forced herself to look away.

> Bastila nodded at the compliment. She wasn’t sure what to make of the woman before her. Meetra had short brown hair, pale white skin, and piercing blue eyes. She was taller than Bastila, and almost a decade older, though she would still be considered beautiful by any empirical measure. She possessed a presence and confidence, along with an enviable natural grace. She was clad in the simple robes of a Jedi Master, but somehow she managed to make even the drab brown cloth seem stylish.
— Source: Ch17 · L3523 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Bastila), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.

> There were other possibilities to consider, as well. The Sith were an aggressive and war-like species; it was possible that Dromund Kaas was just one of many worlds in the Unknown Regions that had fallen under their control over the last thousand years. It was possible—and even likely—that the red-skinned being who had captured Revan had taken him to an entirely different planet, one she had never even heard of. But at least she had a lead. And no matter how slim the odds of finding her mentor, Meetra wasn’t about to give up. She trusted in the Force; eventually it would lead her to him.
— Source: Ch18 · L3879 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Meetra), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.

The Republic had officially condemned slavery, though she knew it still existed under euphemisms like indentured service or lifelong personal attendant. And on Hutt-controlled planets, which were outside Republic jurisdiction, individuals were openly bought and sold like chattel. But somehow what she had encountered on Dromund Kaas seemed much worse.
— Source: Ch20 · L4209 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Meetra), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Revan (Drew Karpyshyn), Chapter Twenty (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Old_Republic_Series_Star_Wars_Old_Republic_03_Revan_Drew_Karpyshyn_epub`).)
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/revan-novel-notes.md:2075`

What it shows (our notes): Ch20 · L4213 · POV Meetra · NARRATION · POST-KOTOR II Meetra (~3,951 BBY): «Multiple escape attempts were met with public execution—a slow and agonizing death according to what Meetra had seen in the official records from Nathema.»
> There was an abject hopelessness in the eyes of the slaves; they were condemned to a lifetime of servitude with no chance of freedom. Even on Hutt worlds slaves could at least dream of one day escaping to the Republic and starting a new life. But in the Sith Empire, slaves had nowhere to run. Every planet would condemn them; at best an escaped slave would be returned to a wrathful owner, or claimed by a new one. Multiple escape attempts were met with public execution—a slow and agonizing death according to what Meetra had seen in the official records from Nathema.
— Source: Ch20 · L4213 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Meetra), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Revan (Drew Karpyshyn), Chapter Twenty (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Old_Republic_Series_Star_Wars_Old_Republic_03_Revan_Drew_Karpyshyn_epub`).)
Our notes: `notes/materials/023-kotor-and-revan/revan-novel-notes.md:2076`

What it shows (our notes): Ch21 · L4469 · POV Meetra · NARRATION · POST-KOTOR II Meetra (~3,951 BBY): «It was possible the Sith had come alone, but Meetra estimated the vehicle could hold as many as six adult humanoids.»
> Arriving at the location only confirmed her suspicions. Sechel’s instructions had led her to an isolated cave on the outskirts of the city—the perfect place for an ambush. A single speeder was parked in a clearing about fifty meters away from the entrance, evidence that somebody else was already there. It was possible the Sith had come alone, but Meetra estimated the vehicle could hold as many as six adult humanoids. There was also the possibility that other speeders had been hidden nearby, meaning she had no idea how many might be waiting for her inside the cave.
— Source: Ch21 · L4469 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Meetra), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.

> The explosions continued as he led them through the twisting passages. They were coming from all sides; obviously the Imperial Guard had the entire stronghold surrounded. Based on the frequency and size of the distant explosions, Meetra guessed they were using an artillery assault to try to breach the walls at multiple locations. Males and females ran past them in both directions, some rushing to join the fray and others scrambling to safety. The unexpected attack had caught Nyriss’s people completely off guard. They were in disarray, their efforts to defend the stronghold uncoordinated and disorganized.
— Source: Ch24 · L5163 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes. POV: narrator (POV Meetra), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.

What it shows (our notes): Ch25 · L5615 · POV Scourge · OWN LINE (attribution) · POST-KOTOR II Meetra (~3,951 BBY): «"A good speech," Meetra said. "But why should we believe you?"»
> “A good speech,” Meetra said. “But why should we believe you?”
>
> “It’s true,” Revan assured her. “When the Emperor broke my will, he looked into my mind, and I was able to see the reflection of his own evil. Invading the Republic is only the first step of his plan. He has become obsessed with power and immortality. The dark side is like a cancer inside him; it grows faster than he can feed it. He has consumed an entire world, but he still hungers. And with his hunger comes an all-consuming fear. He has lived a thousand years; he knows he could live many thousands more. He is terrified of death.”
— Source: Ch25 · L5615 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes.

> The people of Nathema had spent their last days in a terrified and desperate state. They had known it was only a matter of time until the Jedi found them, and the ruler of Nathema—a Sith named Lord Vitiate—had preyed upon his people’s fear. Transcripts of Vitiate’s public speeches were filled with graphic warnings of what the Jedi would do once they arrived. Records confirmed that his speeches had been broadcast and transmitted across the whole of the Empire, sowing the seeds of terror among all the Sith worlds. Vitiate had consciously and carefully driven the people into a state of panic, knowing they would blindly follow anyone who offered hope.
— Source: Ch18 · L3855 — The Old Republic: Revan (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes; Ch18 · L3855 · POV Meetra · NARRATION · NATHEMA ritual (lore). POV: narrator (POV Meetra), about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.

> The explosions continued as he led them through the twisting passages. They were coming from all sides; obviously the Imperial Guard had the entire stronghold surrounded. > Arriving at the location only confirmed her suspicions. Sechel’s instructions had led her to an isolated cave on the outskirts of the city—the perfect place for an ambush. A single speeder was parked in a clearing about fifty meters away from the entrance, evidence that somebody else was already there. It was possible the Sith had come alone, but Meetra estimated the vehicle could hold as many as six adult humanoids. There was also the possibility that other speeders had been hidden nearby, meaning she had no idea how many might be waiting for her inside the cave.

## The Complete Star Wars Encyclopedia, Vol. I (A–G)

> They were known for their speed and for their keen sense of hearing. During the Yuuzhan Vong War, Yavin 13 was devastated and the Gerb civilization was ex­ terminated. Gerbaud 2 Located in the Sepan sector, the planet Gerbaud 2 was the site from which Imperial Admiral Harkov attempted to re­ supply his TIE Advanced squadrons during a rendezvous with the escort carrier Tropsobor following the Battle of Hoth. A united force from the nearby planets of Ripoblus and Dimok attacked and thwarted the transfer operation. GerevickA rude and crude-mannered resi­ dent of the Khoonda Plains on Dantooine in the years following the )edi Civil War, he was a salvager who moved across the grasslands in search of anything of value left behind in the wake of the destruction of the )edi Enclave on the planet. Gerevick was killed when he tried to attack the Exile and her party after they had discovered a wealth of information within the Enclave. GergrisAn Imperial Moff, he was in charge of the Halthor sector during the Galactic Civil War. Geridard The primary planet in the Geri­ dard system of the Doldur sector, it was ' the site of a renowned retirement complex that was rumored to have the best lifelong care in the galaxy. Many corporate officials and other affluent individuals spent their twi­ light years on Geridard.
— Source: scan pages 321–360; Meetra Surik. POV: Meetra Surik.

Our notes: `notes/materials/037-sw-d20-folder/732-web-articles-scan-241-300.md:553`

> His visage is terrible to behold: a decomposing body held together by dark side energies channeled by Sion himself, sustaining his life through the Force, powered by anger and hatred. He is in eternal pain but endures it as a small price for apparent immortality. However, in the end, despite his anger, hatred, and manipulation of dark side energy, the Lord of Pain could not defeat the Jedi Exile. In the Star Wars Miniatures Game, Darth Sion is powerful and resilient. He is a decent melee combatant with Double Attack and a +11 Attack score. His Defense score is a bit low for a character of his cost, but Lightsaber Duelist bolsters him significantly against lightsaber-wielding opponents.
— Source: Web Articles.pdf [Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition (2007)] (scan p; Meetra Surik. POV: Meetra Surik.

He also says: "As powerful as Nihilus' ability is in the short term, the drawback is that it robs the user of almost [all] identity but hunger, which is why is never employed by the Sith Lords of old." > never [being] employed by the Sith Lords of old
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Daily Research — 2026-10-21 session.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Bot's read. The writer built him as the Exile's mirror. His loss to the Exile is a designed thematic counter, the black hole beating the vampire, not proof that anyone at the Exile's power level beats him. It also means his drain "never [being] employed by the Sith Lords of old" is about the cost of the power, not a sign that older Sith couldn't match it.
Our notes: `notes/daily-research/2026-10-21-session.md:52`

> You're like Malachor... it's in you, it's what you are now
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Daily Research — 2026-10-21 session.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Claim 2: if the nexus were strong enough to explain the feat, Meetra would have been crippled at the Trayus Core, so her beating Traya there means the nexus can't be that strong. This is the blog's main argument, and I don't buy it. It assumes Malachor hinders Meetra, but the blog itself quotes the line "You're like Malachor... it's in you, it's what you are now" about her. The Exile is a Malachor survivor and a wound in the Force herself, so there's no basis for saying the nexus hurt her. Azronger's reply holds up better. The Prima Guide says Malachor's energies eroded land formations and mutated wildlife, and the lore treats a nexus as a passive amp whether or not a scene mentions it. The blogger then accepted that he was amped (post 7).
Our notes: `notes/daily-research/2026-10-21-session.md:59`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Claim 3: the "Telos-starved" Nihilus dominated Meetra plus companions with a gesture, so prime Nihilus must be far stronger. Half right. The starving is verified (Tobin). But the evidence for domination is the opening stun and a Force Wave. The opening stun is real (Prima Guide). The Force Wave the blog quotes is labeled "(Cut Content)" in its own citation, and the LP confirms it never made the final game. The bridge-wide TK point is therefore invalid.
  — Source: Daily Research — 2026-10-21 session.

> “He’s quarantined Dromund Kaas,” Meetra said, trying to lead them to the same conclusion. “What if he’s preparing to do the same thing here that he did on Nathema?”
>
> Scourge hadn’t considered that possibility, and it chilled him to his core.
>
> “Is that possible?” he asked. “Nyriss told me the ritual on Nathema took days, if not weeks. And the Emperor had to trick hundreds of other powerful Sith into working with him so he could draw on their power.”
>
> “He’s stronger now,” Revan said. “But even if it’s possible, I don’t think he’ll go that far. At least not yet. He is too patient, too careful. Dromund Kaas is the heart of his Empire and the seat of his power. He has too many valuable resources here to throw it all away. But once he is ready, there will be nothing left to stop him from launching his invasion of the Republic.”

## "Revan's Combat Style -- Short Summary" (DarthAnt66)

> The Handmaiden: **Stance, form, discipline are a means of expression and communication. They speak one's heart and one's devotion to their cause. The methods you use to meet your opponents speak truer than any words can express.**
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Meetra Surik: Combat is not the best way to communicate with others.
>
> So you'd consider slaughter a means of expression?
>
> I agree. It shows how far you are willing to go for your goals.
>
> The Handmaiden:  It was to the Jedi traitor Malak. It was to the Jedi traitor Revan. When Taris was destroyed, it showed Malak's heart through its execution and intent. It was brutal, without finesse... but showed his commitment to defeat the Jedi. **Yet with Revan, there was the same commitment, but it was a subtle thing, like weaving threads in a tapestry, or strokes upon a canvas. He spoke through battle and tactics, in a way one could never do in words. He showed his heart at Malachor V... and finally, at the end of the Jedi Civil War. I believe he was speaking to Malak in that final battle, though few knew it.**
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Meetra Surik: What do you think Revan was saying?
>
> The Handmaiden: Through battle, Revan was meeting betrayal with betrayal - and showing Malak the pain he had inflicted on his master. **Poetry is wordplay that cloaks feelings in riddles. Battle speaks, and its feeling is true. **Revan knew this when facing Malak... and slaying him.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: //www.suspectinsightforums.com/t4507-revan-s-combat-style-short-summary.
Context: A3. Source as labelled by blogger: KOTOR 2 (raw thread.txt line 46)
Our notes: `notes/materials/006-revans-combat-style-short-summary.md:104`

> Meetra Surik: You seem to be anticipating my attacks.
>
> The Handmaiden: **It is the way of the Echani to be able to read their opponents - to know where an opponent is going to strike before it connects, anticipate it, and then strike against them. Echani battles are fought several minutes in advance - in many ways, it is much like the game of dejarik played in the core systems. The most advanced among the Echani are able to predict the course of battles by months, and the most revered are said to be able to predict the path of wars. Only Revan ever demonstrated such skill in war. And even as he slaughtered us, the Echani still respected him.**

## feat and cited-statement extraction

What it shows (our notes): “With the strength Surik lent him, Revan fought the Emperor's control and even influenced the Emperor in return, tempering the Sith leader's desire to restart war and postponing the renewal of conflict with the Republic for almost three centuries.” FEAT/interaction; scaling/connection: Revan and Meetra Surik influenced him.
> Testing Scourge's loyalty, the Emperor ordered him to kill Revan, but stopped him just before the lightsaber reached its mark. The Emperor decided to imprison Revan, prolonging his life indefinitely through a combination of Sith alchemy and technology, and placing him in a specially constructed prison in the heart of the impossible-to-navigate Maelstrom Nebula. Scourge, who claimed to have deceived the two Jedi in order to bring them before the Emperor, was rewarded with the position of Emperor's Wrath, his personal enforcer, and the same immortality that Revan received. Surik's spirit survived the death of her body, however, and remained faithfully at her friend's side while the Emperor fed off Revan's strength and knowledge. With the strength Surik lent him, Revan fought the Emperor's control and even influenced the Emperor in return, tempering the Sith leader's desire to restart war and postponing the renewal of conflict with the Republic for almost three centuries.
— Source: The Old Republic: Revan ; Star Wars: The Old Republic — Flashpoint: "Maelstrom Prison" [per Wookieepedia "Tenebrae" rev 15726991, ref 1,45; exact text match]; per Wookieepedia "Tenebrae" rev 15726991, ref 1,45; exact text match — Tenebrae / Vitiate / Valkorion — feat and cited-statement extraction.

(Source: The Old Republic: Revan ; Star Wars: The Old Republic — Flashpoint: "Maelstrom Prison" [per Wookieepedia "Tenebrae" rev 15726991, ref 1,45; exact text match])

What it shows (our notes): Near-blitzes Meetra and Scourge; lightning killing blow (TOR: Revan) (debate #10)
> She raised her free hand above her head and fired off another burst of lightning. Both Scourge and Meetra threw themselves clear of the deadly electrical bolt, but in doing so gave Nyriss the early advantage.
>
> Before they could recover, she leapt at them. Despite her withered appearance, she moved with the all the speed and ferocity of a dark side warrior in her prime. She landed right between her two adversaries, her blade flashing back and forth in a series of slashes and cuts that immediately threw her two opponents on the defensive.
>
> Scourge barely managed to parry the first wave of her assault, unable to even think about countering with an attack of his own. Another quick thrust forced him off balance and he staggered backward.
>
> Nyriss seized on the opportunity to focus all her efforts on breaking through Meetra’s defenses. The Jedi was clearly overmatched; though she managed to hold her ground, she was forced down to one knee.
>
> In the awkward position her right flank was exposed, and Nyriss brought her blade in to deliver a crippling cut. At the same time, Scourge lashed out with the Force, catching Nyriss flush in the center of her chest.
>
> The air around her began to crackle and grow hot as she gathered herself for the killing blow. Scourge felt the energy building inside her, and he knew he would be powerless to stop it. Nyriss was too powerful; her command of the dark side was too strong.
>
> “Gaze upon me and see your doom!” she declared. “I am Darth Nyriss, Lord of the Sith. I am the conqueror of Drezzi, the destroyer of Melldia, and a member of the Dark Council!”
>
> Scourge braced himself for the end.
>
> Just then, Revan emerged from the cell. He had pulled the hood of his Jedi robe up to cover his head, and he wore the red-and-gray mask, hiding his face.
>
> Just then, Revan emerged from the cell. He had pulled the hood of his Jedi robe up to cover his head, and he wore the red-and-gray mask, hiding his face. A dozen bolts of lightning sprang from Nyriss’s hand, arcing across the room to incinerate her enemies. Instead of leaping back into the cell to avoid the deadly attack, Revan stepped forward to intercept it.
>
> Both hands were held in front of him, his arms fully extended at shoulder height, his thumbs touching and his fingers splayed wide. He drew the bolts of lightning into his waiting grasp, channeling them away from their intended targets and absorbing their power.
>
> “I am Revan reborn,” he said to Nyriss. “And before me you are nothing.” Nyriss’s eyes went wide as Revan unleashed the power of her own attack against her. She tried to throw up another Force shield, but the bolts ripped it apart and continued on unabated. The lightning engulfed her, the intense heat consuming her instantly, leaving only a pile of charred ash.
— Source: (debate #10); (cited by KingofBlades (February 3rd 2020, 9:04 pmMessage reputation : 100% in debate #10) — Legends Feats & Quotes (Star Wars Legends / EU).

What it shows (our notes): Overpowered Revan with lightning but could not break his mind (The Old Republic: Revan) (debate #7)
> The Emperor rose to his feet, his robes smoking and singed where the lighting had struck him. His black eyes flashed red, and he raised both hands high above his head.
>
> Revan knew he was gathering his power to unleash a swirling storm of pure dark side energy, just as Nyriss had done. The Jedi quickly calculated his options. Realizing he couldn’t close the gap between them quickly enough to stop the assault, he gathered his own energy and spread his hands before him, ready to catch and absorb the Emperor’s attack.
>
> A dozen bolts of purple lightning arced from the Emperor toward him. Revan tried to draw them in and contain them, but the Emperor was infinitely more powerful than Darth Nyriss had ever been.
>
> Revan’s body was engulfed in agony as the electricity coursed through his body. His skin began to boil and blister, the flesh of his face melting and sticking to the superheated metal of his mask as the Emperor poured more and more power into him.
>
> Through the haze of indescribable pain, he saw T3-M4 rushing in to help him. The droid let loose with his flamethrower, bathing the Emperor in fire. At the last instant the Emperor cocooned himself in the Force to save himself from being incinerated, breaking his focus on Revan.
>
> The Jedi collapsed to the ground, burned but still alive, the hilt of his extinguished lightsaber lying on the floor less than a meter beyond his grasp.
>
> Almost too weak to move, Revan managed to raise his head just in time to see the Emperor turn on the brave little astromech. A tremor rippled through the air as the Emperor unleashed the full power of the Force against the defenseless droid.
>
> T3 never stood a chance. The little droid exploded into a million pieces, internal circuits and external casing obliterated in a single instant.
>
> “No!” Revan screamed from the ground as bits of his friend rained down on him in the form of unrecognizable shrapnel.
>
> He tried to rise, but his injured body refused to respond. Instinctively, he called on the Force to give him strength and heal his wounds.
>
> The Emperor was approaching him with calm, purposeful steps. Once he reached Revan’s side he calmly bent down and picked up the Jedi’s fallen weapon, igniting the blade.
>
> The healing properties of the Force were powerful, but Revan’s wounds were severe and he needed more time to restore his strength. Helpless, he could only stare up at the Emperor as he raised the lightsaber to deliver the killing blow.
>
> Even as she called to Scourge to help, Meetra was already sprinting toward the far end of the throne room. Scourge hesitated before joining her, taking a moment to survey the situation, memories of his vision of their failure still fresh in his mind.
>
> What he saw was not good. Revan was being electrocuted, his body spasming uncontrollably as the Emperor blasted him with dark purple lightning.
>
> Revan’s astromech launched a jet of flame at the Emperor, freeing Revan, who collapsed to the ground. In retaliation, the Emperor disintegrated the offending droid, strode over to where Revan lay, and picked the vanquished Jedi’s lightsaber up off the floor.
>
> It all happened in the space of only a few seconds. Meetra was moving fast, but she was too far away to stop the Emperor from eviscerating the prone Jedi at his feet.
>
> The Emperor stood in the exact same position as before; it was as if he hadn't even moved. Revan began to sense the oppressive presence of the dark side weighing down on him. The Emperor was trying to crush his will: to dominate and enslave his mind as he had before. This time, however, Revan was ready.
>
> Instead of charging forward, he opened himself up to the Force, letting both the light and dark side flow through him like twin rushing rivers. But instead of focusing or channelling the Force, he released it in its purest form.
>
> There was a brilliant flash as the air between the two combatants lit up. The energy unleashed was powerful enough to send Revan staggering. The Emperor, unprepared and with much of his strength diverted to his effort to dominate Revan's mind, was sent flying backward.
— Source: (debate #7); (cited by NevesYtneves (DC77) — Legends Feats & Quotes (Star Wars Legends / EU).

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): [42] Section: Force Chain
  — Source: KREIA / DARTH TRAYA RESPECT THREAD — QUOTED-EVIDENCE EXTRACTION — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/shootingnova/blog/kreia-darth-traya-respect-thread/96587/.
Posted by: not named in our notes (see thread/notes link).
(Type: Feat / scene or ability)
(Page claim/scaling context: This Force Chain indeed allows the Force to flow easily between the two of them. When either Kreia or the Exile uses the Force to heal or strengthen themself, the other is affected in the same way. However, when one feels pain, the other feels it as well, unless the one who suffered pain is prepared to mentally shield the other from the pain:)
(Actual quoted evidence:)
("I confess its nature eludes me as well. But the bond is strong, and its roots run deep. It seems that at times of stress - and pain, if they catch us unawares, then the pain is transmitted between us. It seems the Force flows easily between us - when one of us manipulates the Force to heal or strengthen ourselves, the other is aided as well. A powerful technique indeed - though, as we have noticed, it has its drawbacks. When battle is upon us, I suspect our minds are prepared enough to shield each other from the pain. I think we shall not have a repeat incident of what happened at Peragus.")
(Source: Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords)
(Named source: Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords)
Our notes: `external/mega-notes/kreia-respect-quotes.txt:378`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): [127] Section: Force Drain
  — Source: KREIA / DARTH TRAYA RESPECT THREAD — QUOTED-EVIDENCE EXTRACTION — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/shootingnova/blog/kreia-darth-traya-respect-thread/96587/.
Posted by: not named in our notes (see thread/notes link).
(Type: Feat / scene or ability)
(Page claim/scaling context: Kreia explains the nature of Force Drain to the Exile:)
(Actual quoted evidence:)
("You give others strength to act, but it is also possible to draw upon the strength of others to increase your own. It is similar to drawing upon the Force as Jedi do, but when it is touched by the power of the dark side... it is something else, something deadly. These Sith we face... they have learned how to do this. It is a technique that has been lost for some time, not seen in the days since the ancient Sith. They can use it to consume other Force Sensitives - and at the highest pinnacle of power, use it to consume anything that lives. They draw upon the connections in the Force, and devour it. That is why they are drawn to Jedi, why it is easy for them to find where they gather - because it is like the smell of blood to them. And they can draw upon the Jedi's own strength to kill them.")
(Source: Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords)
(Named source: Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords)
Our notes: `external/mega-notes/kreia-respect-quotes.txt:1383`

  — Source: KREIA / DARTH TRAYA RESPECT THREAD — QUOTED-EVIDENCE EXTRACTION — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/shootingnova/blog/kreia-darth-traya-respect-thread/96587/.

(Type: Page-only entry — no separate actual quote/source displayed)
(Page text: Partly because the Force was stripped from her, Darth Traya was successfully able to conceal her true appearance and dark side corruption from the Exile and her party throughout the duration of Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords. Neither the Exile nor any of her other party members suspected this until Kreia revealed herself to be a Sith Lord. Even Force Sight, which divulged the Force alignment of others, showed Kreia to be neutral-aligned, until she revealed to Visas Marr that she was in fact dark side-aligned: [panel: panels/kreia/kreia-partly-because-force-stripped-darth-traya-successfully-1.jpg; panels/kreia/kreia-partly-because-force-stripped-darth-traya-successfully-2.jpg; panels/kreia/kreia-partly-because-force-stripped-darth-traya-successfully-3.gif])

> Revan and the Exile were much like you. They truly believed that they could destroy the Dark Lord. I believed them, too.
>
> I would have served them, but my vision told me they would fail - I cut down the Exile and brought Revan to the Emperor as a gift.
>
> Revan, the Exile and I were all Jedi-trained. We all resisted the Emperor. *(NOTE: "His student" is Scourge's framing; she served under Revan.)*
— Source: Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile (Legends): respect-thread layer, read 2026-10-19 — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/shootingnova/blog/meetra-surik-jedi-exile-respect-thread/96944/ ; https://www.suspectinsightforums.com/t171-jedi-exile-meetra-surik-chronological-respect-thread-2019; They were your greatest heroes." /. POV: Kavar, about Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile.
Note (our notes, around the quote): They were your greatest heroes." / "Revan and the Exile were much like you. They truly believed that they could destroy the Dark Lord. I believed them, too." / "I would have served them, but my vision told me they would fail - I cut down the Exile and brought Revan to the Emperor as a gift." / "Revan, the Exile and I were all Jedi-trained. We all resisted the Emperor."  (NOTE: "His student" is Scourge's framing; she served under Revan.)
Posted by: not named in our notes (see thread/notes link).
(S Kreia (K2, Ebon Hawk Force-listening lesson): "You are strong indeed... what you heard were surface thoughts only, but it is something that masters have trained for for years and never learned.")
(S Visas (K2): "Your strength is superior.... it is as I felt."  + KOTOR Campaign Guide: "A Shadow Hand's expertise in cruelty, trickery and the dark side are second only to the Master - for now." (NOTE: so Exile > Visas, Nihilus's #2))
(S Kreia (K2): "It was Visas' Master - he can feel my presence in the Force." / "The time to hide your presence is coming to a close" (Exile's growth becomes detectable galaxy-wide))
(S Kreia (K2): "That crystal is bonded to you... through it, your power is enhanced.")
(S Kreia/Traya (K2, Trayus Core) [K2 verified]: "You are greater than any I have ever trained." / "From the moment you awoke, I have used you.)
(S Prima guide: "your character should be a powerhouse of destruction" (Malachor); Mysteries of the Jedi / SWA Magazine #3 generic form-quotes (NOTE: generic, not about her; discard).)
(S Power Beyond Belief (WotC web article, about the ultra-powerful Sith Lords incl. the Triumvirate): "Only the greatest heroes may ultimately defeat them." (indirect hype))
(S Chris Avellone (author Q&A, image/quote, secondary but authorial): on the Greater Storm Beast: "Sion might have been able to, but the Exile would have a better chance." On Revan: "...unless the Exile pulled some strange Force deafening move, but even that wouldn't keep Revan down for long." (NOTE: author intent leans REVAN > EXILE))
(S KOTOR Campaign Guide: "Traya uses vast telekinetic powers to wield a trio of lightsabers against her, but she is ultimately killed by the person she considered her greatest disciple.")
(S Sion (K2) [K2 verified]: "You are strong... I cannot see as she does, but I know, that in time, you shall surpass her power." / "You are strong... as strong as I had believed.")
(S Zez-Kai Ell (K2): "it has certainly not dulled your instincts, nor the speed at which you learn." Kavar: "I'm impressed with how quickly you've mastered this form. I always knew you were gifted." Vrook: "I don't know how you learnt that so quickly.)
(S Mandalore (K2): "I thought that Revan was a singular Jedi, but now there's you. With all our martial training, battles and ethics, even our greatest warriors are no match for you.")
(S Kreia (K2): "There is nothing more for me to teach you.)
(S Revan (TOR: Revan novel) to Meetra: "I was told you had been cut off from the Force, but I can sense its power in you. I always knew you had great potential, but you have become far greater than I could ever have imagined.")
(S Revan (SWTOR, Revan spirit/holo): "She was a great Jedi. My most trusted ally. History remembers her as the Exile. To me, she was... more.")
(S Scourge (SWTOR, Jedi Knight story): "For a time I worked with the greatest of your Jedi heroes: Revan and the Jedi Exile." / "Revan was both Jedi Master and Sith Lord. His student, the Exile, defeated the Sith triumvirate.)
(S Revan novel (Karpyshyn, TOR: Revan), Revan to Dr. Kreia? no - Revan to Bastila: "Meetra was a powerful Jedi.)

> You carry all those deaths at Malachor within you, and it has left a hole, a hunger that cannot be filled.
>
> In you, we saw a wound in the Force.
>
> In you, we saw the end of the Force.
>
> You are a cipher, forming bonds, leeching the life of others, siphoning their wills and dominating them... What you carry may mean the death of the Force... and the death of the Jedi.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> You feed on it, and you grow stronger. You're like Malachor... killing hundreds, only to grow more and more powerful.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]

(S Atris (K2, Telos) [K2 verified]: "You were strong enough to withstand it once - but few have your strength in such matters, especially if they are unprepared.")
(S Revan novel: "surviving the trauma of Malachor V had given her the strength to defeat Darth Traya and her followers.")
(S Chodo Habat (K2): "It is a planet's worth of pain." Moza: "I do not know how she endures.")
(F Companions mirror her kill impulses (K2 influence lines: Atton, Bao-Dur, Handmaiden, Visas, Mandalore "When one of us is committed to battle, then all of us are committed."))
(S KOTOR Campaign Guide: Vima Sunrider "cautions the Jedi Exile to be mindful of her powers, particularly her aptitude for severing another's connection to the Force." / "The disturbance in the Force is so great that the Jedi Exile instinctively severs herself from the Force before the backlash rips her to pieces.")
(S Revan novel: "Meetra was much closer to the mass-shadow generator than you were.)
(S Traya (K2): "no Jedi ever made the choice you did.)
(S Kreia (K2) long bond speech: "your bond, in many ways, is predatory, an expression of dominance... it is also possible to draw upon the strength of others to increase your own.")
(S Kreia (K2): "Revan was power.)

  — Source: Meetra Surik / the Jedi Exile (Legends): respect-thread layer, read 2026-10-19 — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/shootingnova/blog/meetra-surik-jedi-exile-respect-thread/96944/ ; https://www.suspectinsightforums.com/t171-jedi-exile-meetra-surik-chronological-respect-thread-2019.
Posted by: not named in our notes (see thread/notes link).
(F vs a legion of Sith on Malachor (Prima: "you must face a legion of elite Sith single-handedly"; gameplay))
(F TOR: Revan novel (Dromund Kaas, weakened by the nexus): TK-kills a soldier into a cave wall; Force barrier vs Nyriss lightning ("she threw up a barrier to save herself from the worst of it, but she was still knocked from her feet"; same lightning killed two guards); dodges Nyriss's lightning with Scourge; Lightsaber throw "knocking it from the Emperor's grasp" during the Vitiate fight; resisted the Nathema Void ("The Void will not take me!...)
(W TOR: Revan novel: Revan + Meetra + Scourge vs Vitiate: she is killed by Scourge's betrayal mid-fight (Scourge: "I cut down the Exile"). The fight shows Vitiate overpowering Revan + Meetra before the betrayal (see revan-comicvine-respect.txt / vitiate files: "an amped vitiate got tkd by a massively weakened revan" is the counter-reading). NOTE: author Karpyshyn wrote her as below Revan in the novel.)
(W SWTOR Codex Jedi Entity: "The betrayer always strikes with the best of intentions.")
(F vs Atris (K2, Telos): Campaign Guide: "But Atris's pride is no match for the Jedi Exile's lightsaber prowess.")
(F vs Nihilus (K2, Ravager bridge): Campaign Guide: "When Nihilus tries feeding on this Jedi, his hunger is mysteriously repelled, as if confronted by his exact polar opposite.)
(F vs Sion (K2, Korriban + Trayus): Campaign Guide: "the Jedi Exile convinces the Dark Lord to release himself from his enslaving hatred. Rage and life flee Sion simultaneously".)
(F vs Traya (K2, Trayus Core): Campaign Guide line above; Prima: "Force Wave can stun Kreia's floating lightsabers." Traya's own endorsement above.)
(F vs the three Masters (DS path, K2): "This master is dead...drained of life... it's like an absence in the Force." (NOTE: dark-side path is non-canon; Legends canon is LS female Exile.)

## Verdicts and rankings (living file)

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Common opponents: the Exile beats both. Sion falls 4+ times and is talked into dying; Nihilus needs the Exile + Visas (+ Mandalore) and the Exile's Force-bond trait is his specific counter, so the Exile result flatters Sion relative to Nihilus.
  — Source: Verdicts and rankings (living file).

> I am glad to leave this place…at last.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Darth Sion heals at will, but you can win by striking him down repeatedly.
— Source: Verdicts and rankings (living file).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Reasoning: Korriban: she fights him and wounds him; he heals and lets her go (Kreia had told her to flee). Malachor V: she strikes him down at least four times, and on the last she breaks his will (he admits his feelings for her, she shows him Kreia is using him) and he lets himself die ("I am glad to leave this place…at last."). The game frames her as his conqueror both physically and mentally. Prima guide: "Darth Sion heals at will, but you can win by striking him down repeatedly."
Posted by: verdict file (verdicts.md).

The neutral-rematch lean moves from low to low-medium, the Exile, because the counter is innate to her rather than dependent on Visas.

Note (our notes, around the quote): What changed: Avellone (via the CV RT): "unless the Exile pulled some strange Force deafening move, but even that wouldn't keep Revan down for long" (author intent). In the Revan novel (Karpyshyn), Revan tells her «you have become far greater than I could ever have imagined» (Revan, Ch25, L5535). That line is about her growth since he knew her in the Mandalorian Wars, not parity. The story frames Revan as the main threat to Vitiate. Scourge kills her with a backstab while she faces the Emperor (Revan, Ch28, L6069-6071); that is not combat evidence. The SI majority puts her below Revan (t2584 unread; KingofBlades in t1100: "Meetra was fodder compared to Revan Reborn").

## https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/97466/ ; https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/154767/ ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Tenebrae

> "The ritual had obviously destroyed Nathema, snuffing out all life on the world. Lord Vitiate had offered his people hope, and instead had brought them a fate worse than death—utter eradication of life, existence, and even the Force.
>
> Meetra was no expert on dark side sorcery, but it was safe to assume Vitiate not only survived the ritual, but emerged more powerful than ever. And with the destruction of everyone on Nathema—including his research team—he alone would have known the location of Dromund Kaas.
>
> The plan was both horrifying and brilliant. In addition to becoming more powerful than Meetra could imagine, Vitiate could blame the extinction of his homeworld on the Jedi, further panicking the remaining Sith worlds. Then he could have offered them a glimmer of hope, promising to lead all those who swore loyalty to him to a place where the Jedi would never find them.
>
> If Vitiate had been as cunning as Meetra imagined him to be, he wouldn’t have led his followers directly to Dromund Kaas. Instead, he would have taken them on a long and trying exodus—during which the Sith would have been forced to turn to him time and again for support and guidance, their dependence on him growing until he went from leader to hero to savior. By the time they finally reached Dromund Kaas, they would likely have worshiped Vitiate as a god—all-powerful and all-knowing."
— Source: CITED EXTRACTION: "Tenebrae/Vitiate/Valkorion - Character Overview and Respect Thread - PART 1" — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/97466/ ; https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/154767/ ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Tenebrae; Source named in post: Star Wars: The Old Republic: Revan.
Context: [27]

> “What did you find out?” Meetra asked as Revan extended a hand to help her up.
>
> “It wasn’t just Nyriss who was attacked. The Emperor killed them all.”
>
> “The files you showed me listed five current members of the Dark Council plotting against him,” Meetra said, looking to clarify. “Are you saying the Emperor’s Guard wiped out all five in the space of a single day?”
>
> “I said he killed them all,” Scourge replied. “All twelve members of the Dark Council—even those who weren’t part of the conspiracy. He wanted to send a message no one would ever forget.”
>
> “How is that possible?” Revan asked. “He attacked a dozen of the most powerful Sith Lords in their seats of power simultaneously? How many troops does he have?”
>
> “The Imperial Guard were only unleashed on Nyriss and two others. The Emperor must have assumed they were the ones least likely to answer his summons. The other nine were called together in the hours before the attack to meet with the Emperor at his citadel. None of them left alive.”
>
> “So what’s happening now?” Meetra asked.
>
> “News of the massacre spread quickly,” Scourge said. “As you would expect, the result was chaos. Thousands are fleeing for their lives, fearing we are on the brink of civil war. Others see an opportunity to strike at rivals weakened by the sudden loss of political allies, and armed platoons are roaming the streets.”
— Source: CITED EXTRACTION: "Tenebrae/Vitiate/Valkorion - Character Overview and Respect Thread - PART 1" — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/97466/ ; https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/154767/ ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Tenebrae; Source named in post: Star Wars: The Old Republic: Revan.

> "Scourge rushed forward, hoping to drive Nyriss back into a corner, but she met his charge with an invisible wave of rippling energy. It picked Scourge up and tossed him head-over-heels, sending him crashing to the wall.
>
> Dazed, he looked up just in time to see another bolt of violet lightning catch Meetra in the chest. Like Nyriss, she threw up a barrier to save herself from the worst of it, but she was still knocked from her feet.
>
> “Did you think I would be as easy to defeat as Xedrix?” Nyriss shouted, raising her lightsaber triumphantly above her head.
>
> The air around her began to crackle and grow hot as she gathered herself for the killing blow. Scourge felt the energy building inside her, and he knew he would be powerless to stop it. Nyriss was too powerful; her command of the dark side was too strong."

> Ordinary soldiers would have been chopped down before they could even draw their weapons, but the Imperial Guard were not so easily felled. The first soldier met her charge and parried the first strike with his electrostaff, the resilient metal of his weapon deflecting the energy blade of the Jedi off to the side so that it carved a deep scar in the wall.
>
> The second soldier leapt into the fray, forcing Meetra to fall back a step to absorb their coordinated attack. Slightly farther away, Scourge and Captain Yarri were also already engaged in close-quarters combat, his lightsaber clashing against her electrostaff as they battled in the narrow confines of the entrance hall.
>
> An alarm began to ring out in the hall, triggered by one of the other three soldiers. Before they could join in the fight, Revan thrust his hand, palm up, in the direction of the sealed durasteel doors, blasting them wide open with the power of the Force.
>
> “Into the throne room!” he shouted, rushing forward.
>
> One of the guards battling Meetra broke off and tried to cut Revan off. The Jedi gathered himself and leapt high in the air, tucking his knees in tight to somersault over his opponent. The guard reacted to the unexpected move a fraction too slowly, his electrostaff slicing through the air above his head and missing Revan by only a few centimeters.
>
> Revan landed on the ground and wheeled around to face the other man. He thrust out with the Force, the impact hitting the soldier square in the chest. Instead of sending him flying, it only staggered him back half a step—this close to the Emperor they were sworn to protect, the guards were able to draw on his power to protect themselves."
— Source: CITED EXTRACTION: "Tenebrae/Vitiate/Valkorion - Character Overview and Respect Thread - PART 1" — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/97466/ ; https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/154767/ ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Tenebrae; Source named in post: Star Wars: The Old Republic: Revan.

> "Meetra could sense his suffering. When she had died, she had not become one with the Force. Loyal to the end, her spirit had remained with Revan, an invisible presence hovering just outside his cell.
>
> She couldn’t speak with him; whatever arcane Sith sorcery the Emperor had used to bind Revan in his cell made that impossible. She doubted Revan was even aware she was there. Yet even though she couldn’t communicate with him, she was able to offer aid and support, her power trickling through the energy barrier that surrounded him, a lifeline he could cling to in the dark ocean of his imprisonment.
>
> As the Emperor fed off him, Meetra was allowing Revan to feed off her. Her sustenance strengthened his resolve whenever he grew weak, refreshing and restoring him so he could continue his never-ending mental war.
>
> Because of her, Revan was able to do more than just fight to keep the Emperor at bay."
— Source: CITED EXTRACTION: "Tenebrae/Vitiate/Valkorion - Character Overview and Respect Thread - PART 1" — https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/97466/ ; https://comicvine.gamespot.com/profile/s_w_legend/blog/tenebraevitiatevalkorion-character-overview-and-re/154767/ ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Tenebrae; Source named in post: Star Wars: The Old Republic: Revan.

>

> This argument proposes that the Emperor, even while temporarily weakened at the end of Act 3, is vastly more powerful than his Novel Incarnation. This stems from the fact that Revan Reborn believed he was not a match for the Emperor alone, yet ”With the combined strength of Meetra, Scourge, and even T3, however, he believed they stood a real chance of victory”, Indicating that Scourge would have been a relevant factor in the fight. Conversely, an absurdly more powerful Act 3 Scourge who is far more intimate with the Emperor forces the Hero of Tython to face Vitiate alone, because ”No one else can resist his direct influence.” This indicates that Scourge would have been nothing more than a hindrance to the Hero’s efforts, which is clearly not the case in the Novel. Essentially, the gap between Act 3 weakened Vitiate and SWTOR Scourge is larger than the gap between Novel Vitiate and Novel Scourge, despite Scourge’s massive growth during that span of time. Thus, Act 3 weakened Vitiate receives any feats and scaling Novel Vitiate benefits from.
— Source: Arcann (XSupremeSkillzX) vs Vong Krayt (Azronger) — https://www.suspectinsightforums.com/t2899-ss-the-tyrannical-ten-arcann-xsupremeskillz-vs-darth-krayt-azronger.

> Let me get this straight: Your claim is that Arcann was only blocking a small fraction of Valkorion’s lightning storm, and that the more potent energy that was “whizzing right past Arcann and in completely different directions from his barrier” was incapable of disintegrating or even charring non-force sensitives? The only way you could make this claim is by not considering the following implications from the scaling chain in my opener:
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Darth Nyriss is able to turn non-force sensitives into charred and smoking husks with a single burst of force lightning:
>
> Darth Nyriss is able to incinerate (i.e. to cause to burn to ashes) Meetra and Lord Scourge, two incredibly powerful force users, with a storm of force lightning:
>
> Revan Reborn is able to “easily” catch Darth Nyriss’ storm of force lightning without gathering his energies and redirect it back at her, “instantly” tearing through her force shield and disintegrating her, leaving only a pile of ash:
>
> The Emperor is described as being “infinitely more powerful than Darth Nyriss had ever been.” When Revan Reborn is faced with a lightning storm from the Emperor, he is quickly overwhelmed despite gathering his energies. This indicates that even Novel Vitiate’s casual lightning would be on par with or far beyond Nyriss’ lightning storm:
>
> Let’s think about the implications of this: Nyriss’ lightning is able to instantly tear through force barriers “unabated” and disintegrate powerful force users. Novel Vitiate’s lightning is “infinitely” more powerful than Darth Nyriss’ lightning, which is then quantified through Revan Reborn. Connecting this to the scaling chain in my opener...
>
> And we essentially have a snap of Valklander’s fingers (i.e. his demonstration of power in KOTFE Chapter 5) being vastly more powerful than Act 3 HoT, who is significantly beyond Act 3 Vitiate, who is vastly beyond Novel Vitiate, who is infinitely more powerful than Nyriss, who can instantly disintegrate powerful force users, leaving only a pile of ash.

> She raised her free hand above her head and fired off another burst of lightning. Both Scourge and Meetra threw themselves clear of the deadly electrical bolt, but in doing so they gave Nyriss the early advantage.
>
> Before they could recover, she leapt at them. Despite her withered appearance, she moved with the all the speed and ferocity of a dark side warrior in her prime. She landed right between her two adversaries, her blade flashing back and forth in a series of slashes and cuts that immediately threw her two opponents on the defensive.
>
> Scourge barely managed to parry the first wave of her assault, unable to even think about countering with an attack of his own. Another quick thrust forced him off balance and he staggered backward.
>
> Nyriss seized on the opportunity to focus all her efforts on breaking through Meetra’s defenses. The Jedi was clearly overmatched; though she managed to hold her ground, she was forced down to one knee.
>
> In the awkward position her right flank was exposed, and Nyriss brought her blade in to deliver a crippling cut. At the same time, Scourge lashed out with the Force, catching Nyriss flush in the center of her chest.
>
> An ordinary foe would have been thrown clear across the room, but Nyriss instinctively threw up a Force barrier to protect herself, absorbing and redirecting the brunt of the impact. Even so, Scourge’s attack knocked her off balance just enough to send her lightsaber wide of the mark, giving Meetra the opportunity she needed to scramble away to safety.
>
> Scourge rushed forward, hoping to drive Nyriss back into a corner, but she met his charge with an invisible wave of rippling energy. It picked Scourge up and tossed him head-over-heels, sending him crashing to the wall.
>
> Dazed, he looked up just in time to see another bolt of violet lightning catch Meetra in the chest. Like Nyriss, she threw up a barrier to save herself from the worst of it, but she was still knocked from her feet.
>
> “Did you think I would be as easy to defeat as Xedrix?” Nyriss shouted, raising her lightsaber triumphantly above her head.
>
> The air around her began to crackle and grow hot as she gathered herself for the killing blow. Scourge felt the energy building inside her, and he knew he would be powerless to stop it. Nyriss was too powerful; her command of the dark side was too strong.
>
> “Gaze upon me and see your doom!” she declared. “I am Darth Nyriss, Lord of the Sith. I am the conqueror of Drezzi, the destroyer of Melldia, and a member of the Dark Council!”
>
> Scourge braced himself for the end.
>
> Just then, Revan emerged from the cell. He had pulled the hood of his Jedi robe up to cover his head, and he wore the red-and-gray mask, hiding his face.
>
> A dozen bolts of lightning sprang from Nyriss’s hand, arcing across the room to incinerate her enemies. Instead of leaping back into the cell to avoid the deadly attack, Revan stepped forward to intercept it.
— Source: Arcann (XSupremeSkillzX) vs Vong Krayt (Azronger) — https://www.suspectinsightforums.com/t2899-ss-the-tyrannical-ten-arcann-xsupremeskillz-vs-darth-krayt-azronger.

## Arcann (Mentor: XSupremeSkillzX, Students: KingofBlades and Praxis) vs Darth Tyranus (Mentor: DarthAnt66, Students: Blade_of_Dorin and Cheth)

What it shows (our notes): Meetra Surik: hindered physically and mentally on Malachor V (*TOR: Revan* "Traversing its surface had been agony"), still fought through an army of Sith and beat Sion repeatedly and Traya.
> She had visited Malachor V years after the cataclysm of the mass-shadow generator. Traversing its surface had been agony. Mentally, she had still sensed the anguish of all who had lost their lives there. Physically, the intense gravity of the world had held her in its crushing grip, leaving her gasping for breath. It had been the most awful and horrific experience of her life … until now.
